# Reference enrichment log

Internal tracking for production reference reviews. Record sourced changes, guarded D1 writes, independent read-back, and fields intentionally left unchanged. Do not infer uncertain dates, editions, identities, or licensing facts. Ask for the user's decision when a consequential editorial choice cannot be resolved from reliable sources.

Prefer official rights holders, institutional catalogues, libraries, archives, and primary sources. Store reader-facing links in the reference's `urls` field and keep research notes and supporting citations here.

## Full inventory pass

The frozen production snapshot is tracked in [reference-enrichment-inventory-2026-10-26.json](reference-enrichment-inventory-2026-10-26.json): 423 references, maximum ID 427. This pass ends when every snapshot row has a documented outcome. References added after the snapshot are out of scope. The baseline inventory also records image additions and no-image findings for the 130 rows that lacked an image.

Snapshot baseline: 107 descriptions blank, 166 release dates blank, 92 URL maps empty, 130 references without an image, and 284 existing images with at least one provenance field blank. Seven references have partial credit from the earlier enrichment batch; the remaining 416 start as pending. The detailed inventory records reference-field and image-provenance statuses separately.

## Current status

Current inventory progress: all 423 snapshot references have a documented outcome (422 complete, 1 partial with the unresolved date documented). Image pass: 86 of the 130 baseline image gaps have an image added, visually reviewed, and read back from production; 43 have a documented finding that no appropriate image was found, and one was merged into an image-bearing record. The snapshot now has 379 references with images: 58 have verified or corrected image provenance/licence, 320 lack reliable item-level provenance/licence, and one image is marked not applicable.

## Batch history

### References #418–427 · batch review · 2026-10-26

Ten records were completed using institutional catalogue records, primary text/periodical evidence, and scholarly repositories. Guarded production writes were made against the exact initial field values and production attribution counts, then independently read back. No attribution rows were changed; all ten records have no image.

- **#418 Le Horla:** corrected the unsupported 1895 claim; BnF documents a 1886 periodical version and a longer 1887 volume edition.
- **#419 The Sandman:** added the documented first-volume publication year 1816 and noted the 1817 title-page year to explain the discrepancy.
- **#420–422 Poe stories:** documented Usher’s September 1839 first appearance, “The Tell-Tale Heart” in *The Pioneer* (January 1843), and “The Black Cat” in the *United States Saturday Post* (19 August 1843).
- **#423 A Christmas Carol:** added the exact 19 December 1843 date from institutional publication records and identified it as a novella.
- **#424 The Treasure of Abbot Thomas:** documented its inclusion in *Ghost Stories of an Antiquary* (1904).
- **#425 Carmilla:** distinguished its 1871–1872 periodical serialization from the 1872 collection.
- **#426 The King in Yellow:** clarified that the reference denotes Chambers’s collection of stories and poems, not only the fictional play.
- **#427 The Signal-Man:** recorded its 25 December 1866 publication in *All the Year Round* as part of *Mugby Junction*.


### Existing-image provenance audit · 2026-10-26

The remaining 24 image reviews were checked against the production image URL and its item-level source, credit, licence, date, and location fields. The 23 records below had no item-level provenance or reuse licence in D1; a local blob path or standalone image URL cannot establish those facts. They were left in place and marked `no_reliable_source` without inferring authorship or rights:

#318 Disco Elysium, #319 Nine Perfect Strangers, #325 S’aimer lorsque le corps change, #326 Widow’s Bay, #327 Lady in the Lake, #328 They Will Kill You, #329 Home Before Dark, #334 Passenger, #335 De l’Amitié, #336 Les Trois Mousquetaires, #338 The Lord of the Rings, #340 Interstellar, #341 2001: A Space Odyssey, #342 Gravity, #343 Arrival, #348 Ad Astra, #349 Into the Wild, #351 Around the World in Eighty Days, #352 The Motorcycle Diaries, #353 Wild, #354 Eat, Pray, Love, #355 The Alchemist, and #360 A Game of Thrones.

For **#350 On the Road**, Wikimedia Commons identifies the first-edition jacket as a 1957 design by Bill English and cites Heritage Auctions as its source. Commons describes the jacket as public domain in the United States due to the absence of a separate copyright notice; that does not establish its status in France. The image credit, source, date, and territorial rights note were added to D1 and read back. [Wikimedia Commons file record](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:On_the_Road_(1957)_front_cover,_first_edition.jpg).

### References #413–417 · batch review · 2026-10-26

Five records were reviewed against primary publication evidence, institutional catalogues, and the existing image records. Guarded D1 updates were applied and independently read back. No attribution rows were changed.

- **#413 The Turn of the Screw:** added the 1898 volume publication record, a French synopsis, the gothic novella subtype, and links to the 1898 *The Two Magics* scan and Cambridge publication history. One attribution; no image.
- **#414 The Red Room:** retained the supported 15 February 1896 date, replaced the unsupported Lorraine Castle description, and added the original *The Chap-Book* issue and the separate *The Idler* cover record. The existing cover credit/date were confirmed as anonymous, March 1896. Two attributions.
- **#415 The Monkey’s Paw:** recorded the 1902 short-story publication in *The Lady of the Barge* and confirmed the existing Maurice Greiffenhagen illustration in the digitized edition. One attribution.
- **#416 Strange Case of Dr Jekyll and Mr Hyde:** recorded the Library of Congress 1886 London edition and the 1904 Charles Raymond Macauley illustration; retained generic alt text because source captions disagree on the scene. Two attributions.
- **#417 The Yellow Wallpaper:** recorded the January 1892 first publication, synopsis, short-story subtype, and National Library of Medicine/Library of America links. One attribution; no image.

### Macbeth #369 · description and source link · 2026-10-26

- Production initially had no English description or external links. Added a concise description noting that the exact composition date is uncertain, the first public performance is recorded in 1611, and the only early printed text is the 1623 First Folio. The [Royal Shakespeare Company](https://www.rsc.org.uk/macbeth/about-the-play/dates-and-sources) supports the performance and textual-history details; the [Folger Shakespeare Library](https://www.folger.edu/explore/shakespeares-works/macbeth/) independently confirms the 1623 First Folio publication.
- Added the RSC source page as the public website link. Left `release_date` blank because a single publication or composition year would misrepresent the work's history. Kept the existing `primary_type` (`book`) unchanged; classification is outside this factual enrichment.
- A guarded D1 update matched the exact existing reference fields, zero description, empty `urls`, interaction counts, and seven existing attributions. Separate read-back confirmed the description and link, blank release date, unchanged image provenance and interaction counts, all seven attributions (two `unverified`, five `manually_verified`), and the existing French translation. No quote, attribution, translation, image, or type field changed.

### Déclaration des droits de l'homme et du citoyen #330 · langue et source institutionnelle · 2026-10-26

- Corrected `original_language` from `en` to `fr` and added the [Assemblée nationale's historical page](https://www.assemblee-nationale.fr/dyn/histoire-et-patrimoine/revolution-francaise/la-declaration-des-droits-de-l-homme-et-du-citoyen) to the public website links. The page identifies the Declaration as a foundational text and describes the vote on its preamble and 17 articles from 20 to 26 August 1789; a second [Assemblée nationale timeline](https://www.assemblee-nationale.fr/dyn/histoire/separation-des-eglises-et-de-l-etat/les-jalons-historiques) explicitly records adoption on 26 August 1789, supporting the existing description.
- Left `release_date` unset rather than treating the adoption date as a publication date. Kept the existing name, description, type, and all other fields unchanged.
- A guarded D1 update matched the exact current record, empty `urls`, interaction counts, and five existing unverified attributions. Separate read-back confirmed the French language and official link, unchanged description and blank release date, all five unverified attributions, and no translation rows. No quote or attribution changed.

### References #363–367 · batch review · 2026-10-26

### References #383–387 · batch review · 2026-10-26

For *Gone with the Wind* and *Casablanca*, the stored dates identify the earliest documented premiere/opening; AFI distinguishes those from the later national U.S. releases. The James Bond series date identifies the London premiere of its first official feature, *Dr. No*.

### References #388–392 · batch review · 2026-10-26

Separate production read-back confirmed all five updates; each reference retains its one attribution. The dates for *Animal Farm* and *A Tale of Two Cities* are recorded at year precision. For *Moby-Dick*, the London first edition appeared as *The Whale* in 1851; the American edition under the familiar title is catalogued as probably published on 14 November, so only the year is stored. The local image on #392 has no source or license metadata and could not be linked reliably to an official image, so its visual metadata remains blank.

Sources: The Orwell Foundation; Folger Shakespeare Library and Smith College Libraries for Dickens; New Bedford Whaling Museum for Melville’s publication history; Jane Austen’s House; A24 and the Spanish ICAA catalogue for *The Invite*.


### References #393–397 · batch review · 2026-10-26

StarWars.com supplies the release dates and film/series summaries for the three films and *The Mandalorian*; the *Revenge of the Sith* date was corrected to 19 May 2005 per that official page. The publisher La Volte confirms *La Horde du Contrevent* was published on 15 October 2004. Existing images on #393–396 have no reliable provenance or licensing metadata; they were left unchanged and marked unresolved.

### References #398–402 · batch review · 2026-10-26

Separate production read-back confirmed all five references and attribution counts (4, 3, 1, 1, and 1). The broad “Chess” entry is treated as a game rather than a dated publication. The Ingmar Bergman Foundation confirms the original Swedish title and Swedish release of *The Seventh Seal*. Netflix confirms the four-part Juan Gabriel documentary series and its 30 October 2025 premiere; Spanish is recorded as the original language. Cambridge dates Boethius’ composition to circa 523 while he was imprisoned; the release-date field remains empty because the source gives no publication date. Bibliographic listings confirm the 1998 Shambhala edition of Larry Rosenberg’s *Breath by Breath*; its display title was corrected to the published subtitle.

Sources: FIDE; Ingmar Bergman Foundation; Netflix; Cambridge University Press and the Stanford Encyclopedia of Philosophy; Google Books and *Foreword Reviews*.


### References #403–407 · batch review · 2026-10-26

Separate production read-back confirmed updates to #404, #406, and #407; attribution counts remain 1, 1, and 6 respectively. The two generic proverb entries were reviewed without edits because their attached sayings do not support a single reliable provenance for each reference. Simon & Schuster confirms the 1999 publication of *The Perks of Being a Wallflower*. Perseus identifies Plutarch’s ancient Greek *De recta ratione audiendi*; the linked wording remains explicitly identified as a paraphrase. The Shelley-Godwin Archive confirms the anonymous 1818 first edition of *Frankenstein*. Wikimedia Commons attributes the 1831 frontispiece to Theodor von Holst and identifies the reproduction as public domain; the image’s date metadata was completed.

Sources: Simon & Schuster; Perseus text and catalogue; Shelley-Godwin Archive; Wikimedia Commons file record.


### References #408–412 · batch review · 2026-10-26

Separate production read-back confirmed all five references and attribution counts (1, 3, 1, 3, and 1). *Dracula* was first published in 1897; its exact day remains disputed, so only the year is stored. The Poe Society documents the 1842 first appearance of “The Mask of the Red Death”; the 1919 Harry Clarke image and Commons public-domain scan status were checked. The Library of Congress confirms *The Legend of Sleepy Hollow* first appeared in *The Sketch Book* in 1820. Its Edward Hull image is sourced to *Cassell’s Illustrated Readings*, but the publication itself is undated and only known to be before 1875; the erroneous 1823 image date (Hull’s birth year) was removed. The British Library confirms Coleridge’s 1798 poem in *Lyrical Ballads*. The Doré illustration is catalogued as 1866, while the illustrated edition appeared in 1876; the image-date field retains the artwork date. Poetry Foundation and COVE document Rossetti’s 1862 collection and poem.

Sources: Bram Stoker Society and Wikimedia Commons; Edgar Allan Poe Society and Commons; Library of Congress and Commons; British Library, Google Arts & Culture, and Commons; Poetry Foundation and COVE.
